Title: Histoire de Loango, Kakongo, et autres royaumes d'Afrique; rédigée d'après les mémoires des préfets apostoliques de la mission françoise; enrichie d'une carte utile aux navigateurs
Creator: Liévin-Bonaventure Proyart (title page: 'Par M. l'Abbé Proyart')
Publisher: Paris: C. P. Berton and N. Crapart; Lyon: Bruyset-Ponthus (title page)
Publication Date: 1776 (title page: 'M. DCC. LXXVI.')
Rights: Public Domain (pd-us-by-date: published 1776); the archive.org record carries no rights field, so the imprint date is the basis
Language: fra
Source: archive.org item histoiredeloango00proy_0, full text from the item's own djvu.txt. Fetched directly 2026-11-15 by the Library thread; the byte count of the downloaded file (481,273) equals the size archive.org lists for the djvu.txt.
Provenance note: Extracted unaltered: the item's own djvu.txt is reproduced whole after this header; nothing was stripped, normalized or corrected. Rights basis: published 1776, out of US copyright by date. OCR quality: readable French with the long s read as f; several chapter headings are garbled. A John Carter Brown Library plate stands at OCR lines 1-4. Word count of the host text: 74,936. Line numbers cited in this header are lines of the host OCR; add 12 for this file's own line numbers.
Content note: Two parts. Part 1, the country and its people: Chapitre premier at OCR line 134 to about 7224. Part 2, the history of the French mission: half-title at 7225-7237, 'Chapitre premier. Origine de la Mission' at 7242, eighteen chapters, ending 'FIN' at 13287. Editorial matter: the dedicatory letter at about 50-118; remarks on the map for pilots at 12915-13286; table at 13292-13430; approbation (the OCR reads 26 May 1770) at 13573; privilege at 13587-13670. Letters of the prefects are quoted inside Part 2 and were not mapped.

Les  peuples  dont  nous  parlons 
habitent  la  côte  occidentale  de

xiale jufqu’au  fleuve  du  Zaire  , dont 
l’embouchure  fe  trouve  vers  les  fix 
degrés  de  latitude  méridionale.

remarquable  eft  celui  de  Loango  ; il 
commence  au  village  de  Makanda  :

teur, comme  le  rapportent  quelques 
voyageurs , mais  vers  le  quatrième

ridionale. Il  a vingt  lieues  de  côtes,

Louifa  , qui  coule  fous  le  cin- 
quième degré  cinq  minutes  de  la

Du  Sol  y des  Eaux  & des  Forêts * 

La  terre  en  général  efi  légère  &

d’un  û bon  fonds  : ils  ne  le  cultivent

terre  leur  rende  au  centuple  , & 
fouvent beaucoup  au-delà, les  grains 
& les  plantes  qu’ils  lui  ont  confiés. 
Un  feul  grain  de  mays  en  produit 
jufqu’a  huit  cens  , & communément 
il  n’en  rend  pas  moins  de  fix  cens»
